namespace l3 {
namespace {

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 1. Memory & Alignment Math Tests
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
void test_memory_and_alignment() {
  L3_CHECK(is_power_of_two(1));
  L3_CHECK(is_power_of_two(64));
  L3_CHECK(!is_power_of_two(0));
  L3_CHECK(!is_power_of_two(63));

  L3_CHECK_EQ(align_up(0, 64), 0u);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(align_up(1, 64), 64u);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(align_up(64, 64), 64u);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(align_up(65, 64), 128u);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(align_down(127, 64), 64u);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(div_ceil(100, 32), 4u);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(div_ceil(128, 32), 4u);

  Size out = 0;
  L3_CHECK(checked_add(100, 200, &out) && out == 300u);
  L3_CHECK(!checked_add(static_cast<Size>(-1), 1, &out));
  L3_CHECK(checked_mul(1024, 1024, &out) && out == 1048576u);
  L3_CHECK(!checked_mul(static_cast<Size>(-1), 2, &out));

  const u64 mem_before = memory_stats().current_bytes;
  f32* arr = allocate_array<f32>(default_allocator(), 256);
  L3_CHECK(arr != nullptr);
  L3_CHECK(is_aligned(arr, kDefaultAlignment));
  L3_CHECK_GE(memory_stats().current_bytes, mem_before + 256 * sizeof(f32));
  deallocate_array(default_allocator(), arr, 256);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(memory_stats().current_bytes, mem_before);
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 2. Arena Tests
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
void test_arena() {
  const u64 mem_before = memory_stats().current_bytes;
  {
    Arena arena(4096);
    L3_CHECK_EQ(arena.allocated_bytes(), 0u);
    L3_CHECK(arena.allocate(0) == nullptr);

    void* p1 = arena.allocate(1024, 64);
    void* p2 = arena.allocate(2048, 128);
    void* p3 = arena.allocate(8192, 64);
    L3_CHECK(p1 != nullptr && is_aligned(p1, 64));
    L3_CHECK(p2 != nullptr && is_aligned(p2, 128));
    L3_CHECK(p3 != nullptr && is_aligned(p3, 64));
    L3_CHECK_EQ(arena.allocated_bytes(), 1024u + 2048u + 8192u);

    Arena moved_arena(std::move(arena));
    L3_CHECK_EQ(arena.allocated_bytes(), 0u);
    L3_CHECK_EQ(moved_arena.allocated_bytes(), 1024u + 2048u + 8192u);

    moved_arena.reset();
    L3_CHECK_EQ(moved_arena.allocated_bytes(), 0u);
  }
  L3_CHECK_EQ(memory_stats().current_bytes, mem_before);
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 3. Status & Result<T> Tests
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
void test_status_and_result() {
  Status ok = Status::ok();
  L3_CHECK(ok.is_ok());
  L3_CHECK_EQ(ok.code(), StatusCode::kOk);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(std::strcmp(ok.message(), ""), 0);

  Status short_err = Status::error(StatusCode::kNotFound, "missing key");
  L3_CHECK(!short_err.is_ok());
  L3_CHECK(short_err.is(StatusCode::kNotFound));
  L3_CHECK_EQ(std::strcmp(short_err.message(), "missing key"), 0);

  Status long_err = Status::errorf(
      StatusCode::kShapeMismatch,
      "matmul shape mismatch: lhs=(%d, %d) vs rhs=(%d, %d)", 128, 512, 256, 768);
  L3_CHECK(long_err.is(StatusCode::kShapeMismatch));
  Status cloned = long_err.clone();
  L3_CHECK(cloned.is(StatusCode::kShapeMismatch));
  L3_CHECK_EQ(std::strcmp(cloned.message(), long_err.message()), 0);

  Result<i64> r_val(42);
  L3_CHECK(r_val.is_ok());
  L3_CHECK_EQ(*r_val, 42);
  Result<i64> r_moved(std::move(r_val));
  L3_CHECK(r_moved.is_ok());
  L3_CHECK_EQ(r_moved.take_value(), 42);

  Result<i64> r_err(Status::error(StatusCode::kOutOfRange, "index out of bounds"));
  L3_CHECK(!r_err.is_ok());
  Status extracted = r_err.take_status();
  L3_CHECK(extracted.is(StatusCode::kOutOfRange));
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 4. Intrusive RefCounted & Ptr<T> Tests
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
struct BaseNode : public RefCounted {
  explicit BaseNode(int* dtor_count) noexcept : dtor_count(dtor_count) {}
  int* dtor_count;
};

struct DerivedNode : public BaseNode {
  DerivedNode(int* dtor_count, i64 payload) noexcept
      : BaseNode(dtor_count), payload(payload) {}
  ~DerivedNode() noexcept { ++(*dtor_count); }
  i64 payload;
};

void test_ptr() {
  const u64 mem_before = memory_stats().current_bytes;
  int destroyed = 0;
  {
    auto res = make_ptr<DerivedNode>(&destroyed, 999);
    L3_CHECK_OK(res.status());
    Ptr<DerivedNode> d = res.take_value();
    L3_CHECK_EQ(d.use_count(), 1u);
    L3_CHECK_EQ(d->payload, 999);

    Ptr<BaseNode> b = std::move(d);
    L3_CHECK(d == nullptr);
    L3_CHECK_EQ(b.use_count(), 1u);
  }
  L3_CHECK_EQ(destroyed, 1);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(memory_stats().current_bytes, mem_before);
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 5. Span<T> & SmallVec<T, N> Tests
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
void test_span_and_small_vec() {
  const u64 mem_before = memory_stats().current_bytes;
  {
    SmallVec<i32, 4> vec;
    L3_CHECK(vec.is_inline());
    for (i32 i = 0; i < 4; ++i) {
      L3_CHECK_OK(vec.push_back(i * 10));
    }
    L3_CHECK(vec.is_inline());

    L3_CHECK_OK(vec.push_back(vec[1]));
    L3_CHECK(!vec.is_inline());
    L3_CHECK_EQ(vec.size(), 5);
    L3_CHECK_EQ(vec.back(), 10);

    L3_CHECK_OK(vec.insert(2, 99));
    L3_CHECK_EQ(vec[2], 99);
    vec.erase(2);
    L3_CHECK_EQ(vec[2], 20);

    Span<const i32> sp = vec.span();
    L3_CHECK_EQ(sp.first(2).size(), 2);
    L3_CHECK_EQ(sp.last(2)[1], 10);
  }
  L3_CHECK_EQ(memory_stats().current_bytes, mem_before);
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 6. Half-Precision (f16 / bf16) & DType / BitNet Sub-Byte Tests
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
void test_half_and_dtype() {
  L3_CHECK_EQ(f16_to_f32(kF16Zero), 0.0f);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(f16_to_f32(kF16One), 1.0f);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(f16_to_f32(kF16NegOne), -1.0f);
  L3_CHECK(is_inf(f16_to_f32(kF16Infinity)));
  L3_CHECK(is_nan(f16_to_f32(kF16NaN)));

  const f32 sub_f32 = f32_from_bits(0x35800000u);
  const f16 sub_f16 = f32_to_f16(sub_f32);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(f16_to_f32(sub_f16), sub_f32);

  const f32 snan = f32_from_bits(0x7f800001u);
  const bf16 qnan_bf16 = f32_to_bf16(snan);
  L3_CHECK(is_nan(qnan_bf16));

  L3_CHECK_EQ(dtype_of<f16>(), DType::kFloat16);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(dtype_of<bf16>(), DType::kBFloat16);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(dtype_of<i8>(), DType::kInt8);
  L3_CHECK(is_subbyte_dtype(DType::kTernary2));
  L3_CHECK(is_subbyte_dtype(DType::kInt4));
  L3_CHECK(is_subbyte_dtype(DType::kUInt4));

  Size bytes = 0;
  L3_CHECK(!checked_dtype_bytes(DType::kTernary2, 5, &bytes).is_ok());
  L3_CHECK_OK(checked_dtype_bytes_padded(DType::kTernary2, 5, &bytes));
  L3_CHECK_EQ(bytes, 2u);
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 7. StrView, FnRef, FlatMap, MMapFile, and ThreadPool Tests
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
void test_new_core_primitives() {
  const u64 mem_before = memory_stats().current_bytes;
  {
    StrView sv("  bitnet.b1_58.layer.0  ");
    L3_CHECK(sv.trim() == StrView("bitnet.b1_58.layer.0"));
    L3_CHECK_EQ(sv.trim().find("b1_58"), 7);

    int acc = 10;
    auto add_fn = [&](int v) noexcept { acc += v; };
    FnRef<void(int)> fn_ref(add_fn);
    fn_ref(15);
    L3_CHECK_EQ(acc, 25);

    StrMap<i32> sym_table;
    L3_CHECK_OK(sym_table.put("wq", 1));
    L3_CHECK_OK(sym_table.put("wk", 2));
    L3_CHECK_OK(sym_table.put("wv", 3));
    L3_CHECK_OK(sym_table.put("wo", 4));
    L3_CHECK(sym_table.erase("wk"));
    L3_CHECK(sym_table.find("wk") == nullptr);
    L3_CHECK_EQ(*sym_table.find("wv"), 3);

    const char* path = "log3_ctest_mmap.tmp";
    {
      std::FILE* f = std::fopen(path, "wb");
      L3_CHECK(f != nullptr);
      const char data[] = "0123456789ABCDEF";
      std::fwrite(data, 1, 16, f);
      std::fclose(f);
    }
    auto mres = MMapFile::open(path, true);
    L3_CHECK_OK(mres.status());
    MMapFile mf = mres.take_value();
    L3_CHECK_EQ(mf.size(), 16u);
    auto sres = mf.slice(10, 6);
    L3_CHECK_OK(sres.status());
    L3_CHECK(StrView(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(sres->data()), sres->size()) ==
             StrView("ABCDEF"));
    mf.close();
    std::remove(path);

    auto pres = ThreadPool::create(4);
    L3_CHECK_OK(pres.status());
    ThreadPool pool = pres.take_value();

    SmallVec<i32, 8> buf;
    L3_CHECK_OK(buf.resize(2048, 0));
    pool.parallel_for(0, 2048, 64, [&](Index /*tid*/, Index b, Index e) noexcept {
      pool.parallel_for(b, e, 16, [&](Index /*inner_tid*/, Index ib, Index ie) noexcept {
        for (Index i = ib; i < ie; ++i) {
          buf[i] = static_cast<i32>(i * 2);
        }
      });
    });

    for (Index i = 0; i < 2048; ++i) {
      L3_CHECK_EQ(buf[i], static_cast<i32>(i * 2));
    }
  }
  L3_CHECK_EQ(memory_stats().current_bytes, mem_before);
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 8. BitOps, Rng (Xoshiro256++), Timer, and ScopeGuard (L3_DEFER) Tests
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
void test_bitops_rng_timer_defer() {
  // BitOps
  L3_CHECK_EQ(ctz32(8u), 3);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(ctz64(1ULL << 42), 42);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(clz32(1u), 31);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(clz64(1ULL), 63);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(popcount32(0xF0F0F0F0u), 16);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(popcount64(0xAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAULL), 32);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(next_power_of_two(0), 1ULL);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(next_power_of_two(17), 32ULL);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(next_power_of_two(64), 64ULL);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(bswap16(0x1234u), 0x3412u);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(bswap32(0x12345678u), 0x78563412u);

  // Unaligned little-endian read/write
  byte raw_buf[16]{};
  write_le<u32>(raw_buf + 3, 0xCAFEBABEu);
  write_le<u64>(raw_buf + 7, 0x0102030405060708ULL);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(read_le<u32>(raw_buf + 3), 0xCAFEBABEu);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(read_le<u64>(raw_buf + 7), 0x0102030405060708ULL);

  // Deterministic Rng (Xoshiro256++)
  Rng rng1(12345ULL);
  Rng rng2(12345ULL);
  for (int i = 0; i < 100; ++i) {
    L3_CHECK_EQ(rng1.next_u64(), rng2.next_u64());
  }
  for (int i = 0; i < 100; ++i) {
    const f32 u = rng1.uniform_f32();
    L3_CHECK_GE(u, 0.0f);
    L3_CHECK_LT(u, 1.0f);
    const u32 b = rng1.bounded_u32(37);
    L3_CHECK_LT(b, 37u);
  }
  const f32 g1 = rng1.normal_f32(0.0f, 1.0f);
  const f32 g2 = rng1.normal_f32(0.0f, 1.0f);
  L3_CHECK(is_finite(g1) && is_finite(g2));

  // Substream splitting via jump()
  Rng stream_a = rng1.split(1);
  Rng stream_b = rng1.split(2);
  L3_CHECK_NE(stream_a.next_u64(), stream_b.next_u64());

  // Stopwatch & monotonic timer
  Stopwatch sw;
  u64 t0 = monotonic_now_ns();
  u64 t1 = monotonic_now_ns();
  L3_CHECK_GE(t1, t0);
  L3_CHECK_GE(sw.elapsed_ns(), 0ULL);
  L3_CHECK_EQ(compute_gbps(1000000000ULL, 500000000ULL), 2.0);

  // ScopeGuard / L3_DEFER
  int cleanup_calls = 0;
  {
    L3_DEFER { ++cleanup_calls; };
    L3_CHECK_EQ(cleanup_calls, 0);
  }
  L3_CHECK_EQ(cleanup_calls, 1);

  {
    auto dismissable = ScopeGuard([&]() noexcept { ++cleanup_calls; });
    dismissable.dismiss();
  }
  L3_CHECK_EQ(cleanup_calls, 1);
}

}  // namespace
}  // namespace l3

int main() {
  l3::set_log_level(l3::LogLevel::kInfo);
  L3_LOG_INFO("Running tests/test_core.cpp...");

  l3::test_memory_and_alignment();
  l3::test_arena();
  l3::test_status_and_result();
  l3::test_ptr();
  l3::test_span_and_small_vec();
  l3::test_half_and_dtype();
  l3::test_new_core_primitives();
  l3::test_bitops_rng_timer_defer();

  L3_LOG_INFO("tests/test_core.cpp: ALL 8 SUITES PASSED (0 bytes leaked).");
  return 0;
}


========== ./include/log3/io/checkpoint.h ==========
// log3/io/checkpoint.h
//
// Fast zero-copy model serialization and deserialization engine for the `.l3`
// binary checkpoint format.
//
// Binary `.l3` Checkpoint Format:
//   * Header (64 bytes):
//       - Magic bytes    : "LOG3_MODEL_V01\0\0" (16 bytes)
//       - Version        : u32 (1)
//       - Endian tag     : u32 (0x01020304)
//       - Tensor count   : u64
//       - Meta offset    : u64 (8-byte-aligned offset to TensorMeta table)
//       - Meta size      : u64 (byte length of TensorMeta directory table)
//       - Reserved       : 16 bytes (zeroed)
//   * Tensor Data Block  : 64-byte-aligned payload buffers (mapped via MMapFile)
//   * Tensor Meta Table  : 8-byte-aligned array of `TensorMeta` descriptors
//                          (name, dtype, rank, dims, quant_scale, offset, size)
//
// Zero-Copy Guarantee:
//   `ModelReader` memory-maps the file in O(1) via `MMapFile` and creates
//   Tensors using `Storage::from_mmap`. Gigabyte-scale model weights are mapped
//   into process virtual memory in < 1 ms with 0 bytes allocated in physical RAM.
//   Notice that `meta_map_` keys (`StrView`) point directly into the immutable
//   memory-mapped `TensorMeta::name` array (not into temporary stack copies),
//   so string keys never dangle when `meta_map_` rehashes or looks up entries!
//
// Compatibility:
//   * C++17, no exceptions, no RTTI.

#pragma once

#include <cstring>

#include "log3/core/flat_map.h"
#include "log3/core/memory.h"
#include "log3/core/mmap.h"
#include "log3/core/platform.h"
#include "log3/core/small_vector.h"
#include "log3/core/status.h"
#include "log3/core/str_view.h"
#include "log3/core/types.h"
#include "log3/tensor/tensor.h"

namespace l3 {
namespace io {

struct TensorMeta {
  char name[128];
  DType dtype;
  u8 rank;
  Index dims[8];
  f32 quant_scale;
  Size data_offset;
  Size data_bytes;
};

class L3_API ModelWriter {
 public:
  ModelWriter() noexcept : entries_() {}

  // Register a contiguous tensor to be serialized into the `.l3` file.
  // Copies `name` into a fixed 128-byte inline buffer so caller-owned transient
  // strings never dangle before `save()` is called, and rejects duplicate names.
  Status add_tensor(const char* name, const Tensor& tensor) noexcept;

  // Serialize all added tensors into `filepath` with 64-byte payload alignment
  // and 8-byte metadata table alignment.
  Status save(const char* filepath) const noexcept;

 private:
  struct WriteEntry {
    char name[128];
    Tensor tensor;
  };
  SmallVec<WriteEntry, 256> entries_;
};

class L3_API ModelReader {
 public:
  ModelReader() noexcept : holder_(), meta_map_() {}

  // Open and memory-map an `.l3` model checkpoint from `filepath`.
  static Result<ModelReader> open(
      const char* filepath, bool prefetch = false,
      const Allocator& allocator = default_allocator()) noexcept;

  Index num_tensors() const noexcept { return meta_map_.size(); }
  bool has_tensor(StrView name) const noexcept { return meta_map_.contains(name); }
  Result<Tensor> get_tensor(StrView name) const noexcept;

 private:
  Ptr<MMapHolder> holder_;
  StrMap<TensorMeta> meta_map_;
};

}  // namespace io
}  // namespace l3


========== ./include/log3/core/fn_ref.h ==========
// log3/core/fn_ref.h
//
// FnRef<R(Args...)>: lightweight, non-owning, zero-allocation reference to any
// callable (lambda, functor, or function pointer).
//
// Why not std::function:
//   * std::function may heap-allocate when captures exceed its small-buffer
//     size, pulls in <functional> and RTTI/exception machinery, and is heavy to
//     copy.
//   * FnRef is strictly 2 pointers wide (16 bytes on 64-bit): one opaque void*
//     pointing to the caller's lambda/callable (or holding a raw function
//     pointer directly) and one trampoline function pointer.
//   * Zero heap allocation, zero exceptions, zero RTTI, trivially copyable.
//
// Lifetime rule (same as Span and StrView):
//   FnRef does NOT own the callable object it references. Pass FnRef as a
//   function parameter (e.g., `parallel_for`, graph traversal visitors, tensor
//   init callbacks) where the temporary lambda stays alive for the duration of
//   the call, or bind it to a named lvalue callable. Function pointers (both
//   `R(*)(Args...)` and `R(*)(Args...) noexcept`) are stored directly by value
//   inside `obj_`, so they never dangle even when passed as a temporary.
//
// Usage:
//   void run_loop(Index n, FnRef<void(Index)> body) noexcept {
//     for (Index i = 0; i < n; ++i) body(i);
//   }
//   run_loop(100, [&](Index i) { out[i] = in[i] * scale; });

#pragma once

#include <type_traits>
#include <utility>

#include "log3/core/assert.h"
#include "log3/core/platform.h"
#include "log3/core/types.h"

namespace l3 {

template <typename Signature>
class FnRef;

template <typename R, typename... Args>
class FnRef<R(Args...)> {
 public:
  using CallFn = R (*)(void* obj, Args... args);

  constexpr FnRef() noexcept : obj_(nullptr), call_fn_(nullptr) {}
  constexpr FnRef(std::nullptr_t) noexcept : obj_(nullptr), call_fn_(nullptr) {}

  // Construct from any callable object F (lambda or functor, excluding raw
  // function pointers/references which are handled below by value).
  template <
      typename F,
      typename CleanF = std::remove_cv_t<std::remove_reference_t<F>>,
      typename = std::enable_if_t<
          !std::is_same_v<CleanF, FnRef> &&
          !std::is_same_v<CleanF, std::nullptr_t> &&
          !std::is_function_v<CleanF> &&
          !(std::is_pointer_v<CleanF> &&
            std::is_function_v<std::remove_pointer_t<CleanF>>) &&
          std::is_invocable_r_v<R, std::remove_reference_t<F>&, Args...>>>
  constexpr FnRef(F&& callable) noexcept
      : obj_(const_cast<void*>(
            static_cast<const void*>(std::addressof(callable)))),
        call_fn_(&trampoline_object<std::remove_reference_t<F>>) {}

  // Construct from a plain function pointer (stores the function pointer
  // directly in `obj_`, so temporary function pointers never dangle).
  constexpr FnRef(R (*fn)(Args...)) noexcept
      : obj_(reinterpret_cast<void*>(fn)),
        call_fn_(fn != nullptr ? &trampoline_fn_ptr : nullptr) {}

  // Construct from a `noexcept` function pointer (in C++17 `R(*)(Args...) noexcept`
  // is a distinct type from `R(*)(Args...)`).
  constexpr FnRef(R (*fn)(Args...) noexcept) noexcept
      : obj_(reinterpret_cast<void*>(fn)),
        call_fn_(fn != nullptr ? &trampoline_noexcept_fn_ptr : nullptr) {}

  constexpr FnRef(const FnRef&) noexcept = default;
  constexpr FnRef& operator=(const FnRef&) noexcept = default;

  R operator()(Args... args) const {
    L3_DCHECK(call_fn_ != nullptr);
    return call_fn_(obj_, std::forward<Args>(args)...);
  }

  constexpr explicit operator bool() const noexcept {
    return call_fn_ != nullptr;
  }

  constexpr bool is_valid() const noexcept { return call_fn_ != nullptr; }

 private:
  template <typename Callable>
  static R trampoline_object(void* obj, Args... args) {
    return (*static_cast<Callable*>(obj))(std::forward<Args>(args)...);
  }

  static R trampoline_fn_ptr(void* obj, Args... args) {
    auto fn = reinterpret_cast<R (*)(Args...)>(obj);
    return fn(std::forward<Args>(args)...);
  }

  static R trampoline_noexcept_fn_ptr(void* obj, Args... args) {
    auto fn = reinterpret_cast<R (*)(Args...) noexcept>(obj);
    return fn(std::forward<Args>(args)...);
  }

  void* obj_;
  CallFn call_fn_;
};

static_assert(sizeof(FnRef<void()>) == 2 * sizeof(void*),
              "log3: FnRef must be exactly two pointers wide.");

}  // namespace l3


========== ./include/log3/core/types.h ==========

// log3/core/types.h
//
// Fundamental scalar types used across the entire log3 library.
//
// Design principles:
//   * Use standard fixed-width integer types instead of compiler-specific
//     primitive assumptions.
//   * Keep this header lightweight and independent of platform/runtime code.
//   * Use signed indexing for tensor dimensions, strides, and offsets.
//   * Use unsigned types for memory sizes and allocation byte counts.
//   * Keep f16 and bf16 as storage-only types. Arithmetic and conversions
//     belong in core/half.h and the numerical kernel implementations.
//   * Enforce the representation assumptions required by the CPU backend.
//
// Compatibility:
//   * C++17
//   * No dynamic allocation
//   * No exceptions
//   * No RTTI

#pragma once

#include <climits>
#include <cstddef>
#include <cstdint>
#include <limits>
#include <type_traits>

namespace l3 {

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Platform representation requirements
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

// log3 currently assumes 8-bit bytes throughout its packed tensor formats,
// memory representation, and low-level CPU kernels.
static_assert(CHAR_BIT == 8,
              "log3 requires platforms with 8-bit bytes.");

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Fixed-width integer types
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
//
// These aliases use the standard exact-width integer types.
//
// Exact-width types are important for:
//   * Binary serialization.
//   * Tensor storage formats.
//   * Quantized weights.
//   * SIMD kernel interfaces.
//   * Cross-platform numerical consistency.
//

using i8  = std::int8_t;
using i16 = std::int16_t;
using i32 = std::int32_t;
using i64 = std::int64_t;

using u8  = std::uint8_t;
using u16 = std::uint16_t;
using u32 = std::uint32_t;
using u64 = std::uint64_t;

// Raw 8-bit storage unit.
//
// This remains an integer alias rather than std::byte to preserve convenient
// compatibility with packed tensor formats and existing log3 interfaces.
using byte = u8;

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Platform-sized integer types
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
//
// usize:
//   Represents the size of an object or memory allocation.
//   Equivalent to std::size_t.
//
// isize:
//   Represents pointer differences.
//   Equivalent to std::ptrdiff_t.
//
// These types are commonly pointer-sized, but their actual guarantees come
// from the C++ standard rather than an assumption that sizeof(pointer) matches.
//

using usize = std::size_t;
using isize = std::ptrdiff_t;

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Floating-point types
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

using f32 = float;
using f64 = double;

// log3 numerical kernels require IEEE-754 floating-point representations.
//
// Size assertions alone are insufficient because a floating-point type can
// have the expected size without having the expected representation.

static_assert(sizeof(f32) == 4,
              "log3 requires 32-bit f32 storage.");

static_assert(sizeof(f64) == 8,
              "log3 requires 64-bit f64 storage.");

static_assert(std::numeric_limits<f32>::is_iec559,
              "log3 requires IEEE-754 f32.");

static_assert(std::numeric_limits<f64>::is_iec559,
              "log3 requires IEEE-754 f64.");

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Tensor-facing integer types
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
//
// Index:
//   Signed integer used for tensor dimensions, strides, and element offsets.
//
//   Signed indexing allows negative strides and avoids unsigned underflow
//   during index arithmetic.
//
// Size:
//   Unsigned integer used for allocation sizes and byte counts.
//
//   Keep indexing arithmetic and allocation-size arithmetic conceptually
//   separate. Conversions between them must be validated.
//

using Index = i64;
using Size  = usize;

// Numeric limits for tensor index arithmetic.
//
// Prefer std::numeric_limits over implementation-provided integer macros.
// These expressions are constexpr and require no runtime initialization.

inline constexpr Index kMaxIndex =
    std::numeric_limits<Index>::max();

inline constexpr Index kMinIndex =
    std::numeric_limits<Index>::min();

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Half-precision storage types
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
//
// f16:
//   IEEE-754 binary16 storage representation.
//   1 sign bit, 5 exponent bits, 10 mantissa bits.
//
// bf16:
//   Brain floating-point 16-bit storage representation.
//   1 sign bit, 8 exponent bits, 7 mantissa bits.
//
// Both types are storage containers only.
//
// IMPORTANT:
//   These structures do not implement floating-point arithmetic.
//   Conversion and arithmetic must be implemented separately in core/half.h
//   and numerical kernels.
//
// The bits member stores the raw 16-bit representation.
//

struct f16 {
  u16 bits;

  // Construct a storage value from its raw binary16 representation.
  static constexpr f16 from_bits(u16 value) noexcept {
    return f16{value};
  }

  // Return the raw binary16 representation.
  constexpr u16 to_bits() const noexcept {
    return bits;
  }
};

struct bf16 {
  u16 bits;

  // Construct a storage value from its raw bfloat16 representation.
  static constexpr bf16 from_bits(u16 value) noexcept {
    return bf16{value};
  }

  // Return the raw bfloat16 representation.
  constexpr u16 to_bits() const noexcept {
    return bits;
  }
};

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Compile-time representation validation
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
//
// Integer aliases must retain their expected widths and signedness.
// These checks catch unsupported or unexpected platform configurations early.
//

static_assert(sizeof(i8) == 1 && sizeof(u8) == 1,
              "log3 requires 8-bit integer types.");

static_assert(sizeof(i16) == 2 && sizeof(u16) == 2,
              "log3 requires 16-bit integer types.");

static_assert(sizeof(i32) == 4 && sizeof(u32) == 4,
              "log3 requires 32-bit integer types.");

static_assert(sizeof(i64) == 8 && sizeof(u64) == 8,
              "log3 requires 64-bit integer types.");

static_assert(std::is_signed<i8>::value &&
                  std::is_signed<i16>::value &&
                  std::is_signed<i32>::value &&
                  std::is_signed<i64>::value,
              "log3 signed integer aliases must be signed.");

static_assert(!std::is_signed<u8>::value &&
                  !std::is_signed<u16>::value &&
                  !std::is_signed<u32>::value &&
                  !std::is_signed<u64>::value,
              "log3 unsigned integer aliases must be unsigned.");

// Tensor-facing types must preserve their intended signedness.

static_assert(std::is_signed<Index>::value,
              "log3 Index must be signed.");

static_assert(!std::is_signed<Size>::value,
              "log3 Size must be unsigned.");

static_assert(sizeof(Index) == 8,
              "log3 Index must be 64 bits.");

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Half-precision storage validation
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

static_assert(sizeof(f16) == 2,
              "log3 f16 storage must be exactly 2 bytes.");

static_assert(sizeof(bf16) == 2,
              "log3 bf16 storage must be exactly 2 bytes.");

static_assert(alignof(f16) == alignof(u16),
              "log3 f16 alignment must match u16.");

static_assert(alignof(bf16) == alignof(u16),
              "log3 bf16 alignment must match u16.");

static_assert(std::is_standard_layout<f16>::value &&
                  std::is_standard_layout<bf16>::value,
              "log3 half storage types must have standard layout.");

static_assert(std::is_trivially_copyable<f16>::value &&
                  std::is_trivially_copyable<bf16>::value,
              "log3 half storage types must be trivially copyable.");

}  // namespace l3


========== ./include/log3/core/rng.h ==========
// log3/core/rng.h
//
// Fast, deterministic, cross-platform pseudo-random number generation (Xoshiro256++)
// and statistical distributions for weight initialization, dropout, stochastic
// rounding, and LLM token sampling.
//
// Why not <random>:
//   * std::mt19937_64 has a 2,504-byte state struct (destroying L1 cache),
//     whereas Rng (Xoshiro256++) is 32 bytes (half a cache line) and 3x-4x
//     faster per 64-bit draw.
//   * std::uniform_real_distribution and std::normal_distribution are NOT
//     standardized across libstdc++ (Linux), libc++ (macOS/Android), and MSVC
//     (Windows) — the same seed produces DIFFERENT random tensors on different
//     OSes! `l3::Rng` uses exact IEEE-754 bit math so a seed produces
//     bit-identical tensors and token streams on every OS and CPU architecture.
//   * Supports `.jump()` (advances state by 2^128 steps in O(1)) so each thread
//     in `ThreadPool::parallel_for` gets a provably non-overlapping substream.
//
// Compatibility:
//   * C++17, header-only, no exceptions, no RTTI.

#pragma once

#include <cmath>

#include "log3/core/assert.h"
#include "log3/core/half.h"
#include "log3/core/platform.h"
#include "log3/core/str_view.h"
#include "log3/core/types.h"

namespace l3 {

class Rng {
 public:
  // Initialize with a 64-bit seed (expanded via SplitMix64 so even seed=0
  // produces a high-entropy non-zero state).
  explicit constexpr Rng(u64 seed = 0x853c49e6748fea9bULL) noexcept
      : s_{0, 0, 0, 0}, has_spare_normal_(false), spare_normal_(0.0f) {
    reseed(seed);
  }

  constexpr void reseed(u64 seed) noexcept {
    u64 z = seed;
    for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) {
      z += 0x9e3779b97f4a7c15ULL;
      s_[i] = hash_u64(z);
    }
    has_spare_normal_ = false;
    spare_normal_ = 0.0f;
  }

  // --- core integer draws (Xoshiro256++ by Blackman & Vigna) -----------------
  constexpr u64 next_u64() noexcept {
    const u64 result = rotl(s_[0] + s_[3], 23) + s_[0];
    const u64 t = s_[1] << 17;

    s_[2] ^= s_[0];
    s_[3] ^= s_[1];
    s_[1] ^= s_[2];
    s_[0] ^= s_[3];

    s_[2] ^= t;
    s_[3] = rotl(s_[3], 45);
    return result;
  }

  constexpr u32 next_u32() noexcept {
    return static_cast<u32>(next_u64() >> 32);
  }

  // Unbiased bounded integer in [0, bound) using Lemire's fast divisionless
  // rejection method where possible. Precondition: bound > 0.
  u32 bounded_u32(u32 bound) noexcept {
    L3_DCHECK_GT(bound, 0u);
    u64 x = next_u32();
    u64 m = x * static_cast<u64>(bound);
    u32 l = static_cast<u32>(m);
    if (L3_UNLIKELY(l < bound)) {
      const u32 threshold = (0u - bound) % bound;
      while (l < threshold) {
        x = next_u32();
        m = x * static_cast<u64>(bound);
        l = static_cast<u32>(m);
      }
    }
    return static_cast<u32>(m >> 32);
  }

  // --- floating-point draws (bit-exact across x86-64 and ARM64) --------------

  // Uniform f32 in [0.0f, 1.0f) with 24 bits of mantissa precision.
  L3_FORCE_INLINE f32 uniform_f32() noexcept {
    // 24 random bits * 2^-24 (0x1.0p-24f == 5.9604644775390625e-8f).
    return static_cast<f32>(next_u32() >> 8) * (1.0f / 16777216.0f);
  }

  // Uniform f32 in [low, high).
  L3_FORCE_INLINE f32 uniform_f32(f32 low, f32 high) noexcept {
    return low + (high - low) * uniform_f32();
  }

  // Uniform f64 in [0.0, 1.0) with 53 bits of mantissa precision.
  L3_FORCE_INLINE f64 uniform_f64() noexcept {
    return static_cast<f64>(next_u64() >> 11) * (1.0 / 9007199254740992.0);
  }

  // Standard normal distribution N(0, 1) using Marsaglia's polar method
  // (avoids trigonometric sin/cos calls and caches the second variate).
  f32 normal_f32(f32 mean = 0.0f, f32 stddev = 1.0f) noexcept {
    if (has_spare_normal_) {
      has_spare_normal_ = false;
      return mean + stddev * spare_normal_;
    }

    f32 u = 0.0f;
    f32 v = 0.0f;
    f32 s = 0.0f;
    do {
      u = uniform_f32() * 2.0f - 1.0f;
      v = uniform_f32() * 2.0f - 1.0f;
      s = u * u + v * v;
    } while (s >= 1.0f || s == 0.0f);

    const f32 mul = std::sqrt(-2.0f * std::log(s) / s);
    spare_normal_ = v * mul;
    has_spare_normal_ = true;
    return mean + stddev * (u * mul);
  }

  // Bernoulli trial: returns true with probability `p` in [0.0, 1.0].
  L3_FORCE_INLINE bool bernoulli(f32 p) noexcept {
    if (p <= 0.0f) return false;
    if (p >= 1.0f) return true;
    return uniform_f32() < p;
  }

  // --- parallel stream splitting ---------------------------------------------
  // Advance state by 2^128 calls to next_u64(). Use this to spawn independent
  // per-thread Rng instances inside ThreadPool::parallel_for.
  constexpr void jump() noexcept {
    constexpr u64 kJump[4] = {
        0x180ec6d33cfd0abaULL,
        0xd5a61266f0c9392cULL,
        0xa9582618e03fc9aaULL,
        0x39abdc4529b1661cULL,
    };
    u64 s0 = 0, s1 = 0, s2 = 0, s3 = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) {
      for (int b = 0; b < 64; ++b) {
        if ((kJump[i] & (1ULL << b)) != 0) {
          s0 ^= s_[0];
          s1 ^= s_[1];
          s2 ^= s_[2];
          s3 ^= s_[3];
        }
        next_u64();
      }
    }
    s_[0] = s0;
    s_[1] = s1;
    s_[2] = s2;
    s_[3] = s3;
    has_spare_normal_ = false;
  }

  // Return a new Rng whose sequence is offset by `stream_index` jumps (each
  // jump is 2^128 steps).
  constexpr Rng split(Index stream_index = 1) const noexcept {
    Rng copy = *this;
    copy.has_spare_normal_ = false;
    for (Index i = 0; i < stream_index; ++i) {
      copy.jump();
    }
    return copy;
  }

 private:
  static constexpr u64 rotl(u64 x, int k) noexcept {
    return (x << k) | (x >> (64 - k));
  }

  u64 s_[4];
  bool has_spare_normal_;
  f32 spare_normal_;
};

}  // namespace l3


========== ./include/log3/core/bit_ops.h ==========
// log3/core/bit_ops.h
//
// Low-level bit manipulation, compiler intrinsics, endian byte-swapping, and
// unaligned little-endian memory load/store helpers.
//
// Why not <bit> (C++20) or manual loops:
//   * log3 targets C++17, where <bit> (std::popcount, std::countr_zero,
//     std::bit_ceil, std::byteswap) does not exist.
//   * Every function here maps to a single CPU instruction on x86-64 and ARM64
//     (TZCNT/BSF, LZCNT/BSR, POPCNT/CNT, BSWAP/REV).
//   * Model loaders (.gguf, .safetensors, .l3) and tokenizer files are stored
//     in little-endian byte order at arbitrary byte offsets inside MMapFile.
//     `read_le<T>` and `write_le<T>` perform safe, aliasing-clean (`memcpy`)
//     loads and stores that compile to a single unaligned `MOV` instruction on
//     x86-64 and ARM64.
//
// Compatibility:
//   * C++17, header-only, no exceptions, no RTTI.

#pragma once

#include <cstring>
#include <type_traits>

#include "log3/core/assert.h"
#include "log3/core/platform.h"
#include "log3/core/types.h"

#if L3_MSVC_ABI
#include <intrin.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#endif

namespace l3 {

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Count trailing zeros (ctz)
//   Precondition: x != 0 (calling ctz on 0 is undefined in hardware intrinsics;
//   checked via L3_DCHECK in debug builds).
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
L3_FORCE_INLINE int ctz32(u32 x) noexcept {
  L3_DCHECK_NE(x, 0u);
#if L3_MSVC_ABI && !L3_COMPILER_CLANG
  unsigned long index = 0;
  ::_BitScanForward(&index, x);
  return static_cast<int>(index);
#else
  return __builtin_ctz(x);
#endif
}

L3_FORCE_INLINE int ctz64(u64 x) noexcept {
  L3_DCHECK_NE(x, 0ULL);
#if L3_MSVC_ABI && !L3_COMPILER_CLANG
#if L3_64BIT
  unsigned long index = 0;
  ::_BitScanForward64(&index, x);
  return static_cast<int>(index);
#else
  const u32 lo = static_cast<u32>(x);
  if (lo != 0) return ctz32(lo);
  return 32 + ctz32(static_cast<u32>(x >> 32));
#endif
#else
  return __builtin_ctzll(x);
#endif
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Count leading zeros (clz)
//   Precondition: x != 0.
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
L3_FORCE_INLINE int clz32(u32 x) noexcept {
  L3_DCHECK_NE(x, 0u);
#if L3_MSVC_ABI && !L3_COMPILER_CLANG
  unsigned long index = 0;
  ::_BitScanReverse(&index, x);
  return 31 - static_cast<int>(index);
#else
  return __builtin_clz(x);
#endif
}

L3_FORCE_INLINE int clz64(u64 x) noexcept {
  L3_DCHECK_NE(x, 0ULL);
#if L3_MSVC_ABI && !L3_COMPILER_CLANG
#if L3_64BIT
  unsigned long index = 0;
  ::_BitScanReverse64(&index, x);
  return 63 - static_cast<int>(index);
#else
  const u32 hi = static_cast<u32>(x >> 32);
  if (hi != 0) return clz32(hi);
  return 32 + clz32(static_cast<u32>(x));
#endif
#else
  return __builtin_clzll(x);
#endif
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Population count (number of set 1-bits)
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
L3_FORCE_INLINE int popcount32(u32 x) noexcept {
#if L3_MSVC_ABI && !L3_COMPILER_CLANG
  // Portable SWAR fallback on MSVC so it works even without /arch:AVX
  x = x - ((x >> 1) & 0x55555555u);
  x = (x & 0x33333333u) + ((x >> 2) & 0x33333333u);
  return static_cast<int>((((x + (x >> 4)) & 0x0F0F0F0Fu) * 0x01010101u) >> 24);
#else
  return __builtin_popcount(x);
#endif
}

L3_FORCE_INLINE int popcount64(u64 x) noexcept {
#if L3_MSVC_ABI && !L3_COMPILER_CLANG
  x = x - ((x >> 1) & 0x5555555555555555ULL);
  x = (x & 0x3333333333333333ULL) + ((x >> 2) & 0x3333333333333333ULL);
  return static_cast<int>(
      (((x + (x >> 4)) & 0x0F0F0F0F0F0F0F0FULL) * 0x0101010101010101ULL) >> 56);
#else
  return __builtin_popcountll(x);
#endif
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Power-of-two & log2 helpers (for hash tables, arenas, FFT/tiling blocks)
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

// Number of bits needed to represent x (0 -> 0, 1 -> 1, 2 -> 2, 7 -> 3).
L3_FORCE_INLINE int bit_width64(u64 x) noexcept {
  return x == 0 ? 0 : (64 - clz64(x));
}

// Floor(log2(x)) for x > 0.
L3_FORCE_INLINE int log2_floor64(u64 x) noexcept {
  L3_DCHECK_NE(x, 0ULL);
  return 63 - clz64(x);
}

// Smallest power of two >= x (for x == 0 or x == 1, returns 1).
// Returns 0 on overflow (when x > 2^63).
L3_FORCE_INLINE u64 next_power_of_two(u64 x) noexcept {
  if (x <= 1) return 1;
  if (L3_UNLIKELY(x > (1ULL << 63))) return 0;
  return 1ULL << (64 - clz64(x - 1));
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Byte-order reversal (BSWAP / REV)
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
L3_FORCE_INLINE u16 bswap16(u16 x) noexcept {
#if L3_MSVC_ABI && !L3_COMPILER_CLANG
  return ::_byteswap_ushort(x);
#else
  return __builtin_bswap16(x);
#endif
}

L3_FORCE_INLINE u32 bswap32(u32 x) noexcept {
#if L3_MSVC_ABI && !L3_COMPILER_CLANG
  return ::_byteswap_ulong(x);
#else
  return __builtin_bswap32(x);
#endif
}

L3_FORCE_INLINE u64 bswap64(u64 x) noexcept {
#if L3_MSVC_ABI && !L3_COMPILER_CLANG
  return ::_byteswap_uint64(x);
#else
  return __builtin_bswap64(x);
#endif
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Unaligned little-endian memory load/store (zero UB, compiles to 1 MOV on
// x86-64 and ARM64). Used heavily by .gguf / .safetensors / .l3 parsers.
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
template <typename T>
L3_FORCE_INLINE T read_le(const void* src) noexcept {
  static_assert(std::is_trivially_copyable_v<T>,
                "read_le<T>: T must be trivially copyable.");
  static_assert(sizeof(T) == 1 || sizeof(T) == 2 || sizeof(T) == 4 ||
                    sizeof(T) == 8,
                "read_le<T>: T must be 1, 2, 4, or 8 bytes wide.");
  T val{};
  std::memcpy(&val, src, sizeof(T));
#if defined(__BYTE_ORDER__) && (__BYTE_ORDER__ == __ORDER_BIG_ENDIAN__)
  if constexpr (sizeof(T) == 2) {
    u16 raw = 0;
    std::memcpy(&raw, &val, 2);
    raw = bswap16(raw);
    std::memcpy(&val, &raw, 2);
  } else if constexpr (sizeof(T) == 4) {
    u32 raw = 0;
    std::memcpy(&raw, &val, 4);
    raw = bswap32(raw);
    std::memcpy(&val, &raw, 4);
  } else if constexpr (sizeof(T) == 8) {
    u64 raw = 0;
    std::memcpy(&raw, &val, 8);
    raw = bswap64(raw);
    std::memcpy(&val, &raw, 8);
  }
#endif
  return val;
}

template <typename T>
L3_FORCE_INLINE void write_le(void* dst, T val) noexcept {
  static_assert(std::is_trivially_copyable_v<T>,
                "write_le<T>: T must be trivially copyable.");
  static_assert(sizeof(T) == 1 || sizeof(T) == 2 || sizeof(T) == 4 ||
                    sizeof(T) == 8,
                "write_le<T>: T must be 1, 2, 4, or 8 bytes wide.");
#if defined(__BYTE_ORDER__) && (__BYTE_ORDER__ == __ORDER_BIG_ENDIAN__)
  if constexpr (sizeof(T) == 2) {
    u16 raw = 0;
    std::memcpy(&raw, &val, 2);
    raw = bswap16(raw);
    std::memcpy(&val, &raw, 2);
  } else if constexpr (sizeof(T) == 4) {
    u32 raw = 0;
    std::memcpy(&raw, &val, 4);
    raw = bswap32(raw);
    std::memcpy(&val, &raw, 4);
  } else if constexpr (sizeof(T) == 8) {
    u64 raw = 0;
    std::memcpy(&raw, &val, 8);
    raw = bswap64(raw);
    std::memcpy(&val, &raw, 8);
  }
#endif
  std::memcpy(dst, &val, sizeof(T));
}

}  // namespace l3


========== ./include/log3/core/mmap.h ==========
// log3/core/mmap.h
//
// MMapFile: cross-platform read-only memory-mapped file for zero-copy model
// weight loading (.gguf, .safetensors, .l3).
//
// Why MMapFile belongs in log3/core:
//   * BitNet b1.58 and modern LLM checkpoints are hundreds of megabytes to
//     tens of gigabytes. Reading them via std::ifstream or fread() duplicates
//     the entire model in RAM and delays startup by seconds.
//   * MMapFile maps the file directly into the process address space via the
//     OS page cache (POSIX mmap / Windows CreateFileMappingW) in < 1 ms with
//     zero heap allocations.
//   * RefCounted-compatible or standalone move-only RAII object. Exposes
//     `ConstByteSpan`, `advise_sequential()`, `advise_willneed()`, and
//     checked sub-slice helpers for safe header/tensor parsing.
//
// Compatibility:
//   * Windows (CreateFileA / CreateFileMappingW / MapViewOfFile / VirtualLock)
//   * Linux, Android, macOS (open / fstat / mmap / madvise)
//   * C++17, no exceptions, no RTTI

#pragma once

#include "log3/core/platform.h"
#include "log3/core/span.h"
#include "log3/core/status.h"
#include "log3/core/types.h"

namespace l3 {

enum class MMapAdvice : u8 {
  kNormal = 0,      // Default OS paging behavior
  kSequential = 1,  // Aggressive read-ahead (scanning model headers/weights)
  kRandom = 2,      // Disable read-ahead (random access into large files)
  kWillNeed = 3,    // Prefetch pages into physical RAM asynchronously
  kDontNeed = 4,    // Hint that pages can be evicted from physical RAM
};

class L3_API MMapFile {
 public:
  MMapFile() noexcept
      : data_(nullptr), size_(0), os_file_handle_(-1), os_map_handle_(nullptr) {}

  MMapFile(const MMapFile&) = delete;
  MMapFile& operator=(const MMapFile&) = delete;

  MMapFile(MMapFile&& other) noexcept;
  MMapFile& operator=(MMapFile&& other) noexcept;

  ~MMapFile() noexcept { close(); }

  // Open `path` and map its entire contents read-only into virtual memory.
  // Fails with:
  //   * kInvalidArgument if path is null/empty or the file is 0 bytes.
  //   * kNotFound if the file does not exist.
  //   * kIoError if mapping fails.
  static Result<MMapFile> open(const char* path,
                               bool prefetch = false) noexcept;

  // Unmap the file and release OS descriptors. Safe to call multiple times.
  void close() noexcept;

  // Provide paging hints to the OS kernel (madvise / PrefetchVirtualMemory).
  Status advise(MMapAdvice advice, Size offset = 0,
                Size length = 0) const noexcept;

  // --- observers -------------------------------------------------------------
  bool is_open() const noexcept { return data_ != nullptr; }
  const byte* data() const noexcept { return data_; }
  Size size() const noexcept { return size_; }

  ConstByteSpan bytes() const noexcept {
    return ConstByteSpan(data_, static_cast<Index>(size_));
  }

  // Checked sub-slice of `[offset, offset + length)`.
  // Returns kOutOfRange if the slice exceeds the mapped file size.
  Result<ConstByteSpan> slice(Size offset, Size length) const noexcept;

 private:
  const byte* data_;
  Size size_;
  isize os_file_handle_;  // fd on POSIX, intptr_t HANDLE on Windows
  void* os_map_handle_;   // mapping HANDLE on Windows, unused on POSIX
};

}  // namespace l3


========== ./include/log3/core/thread_pool.h ==========
// log3/core/thread_pool.h
//
// Low-latency data-parallel thread pool for BitNet ternary/INT4 GEMM, GEMV,
// attention heads, and elementwise tensor kernels.
//
// Why a custom ThreadPool instead of std::async / OpenMP:
//   * Zero per-dispatch heap allocation: tasks are dispatched via `FnRef`
//     (2 pointers wide) and an atomic work-stealing counter.
//   * Hybrid spin-then-sleep barrier: worker threads briefly spin with
//     CPU pause/yield instructions (`_mm_pause` / `yield`) between consecutive
//     layers of a transformer pass, avoiding 5-15 us OS futex/condvar wake-up
//     latency during token generation! If no new work arrives within the spin
//     window, workers sleep cleanly on an OS condition variable (0% idle CPU).
//   * Dynamic chunk stealing: `parallel_for(0, end, grain_size, fn)` divides
//      `[begin, end)` into chunks of `grain_size` and lets workers claim chunks
//     via a single cache-line-isolated `std::atomic<Index>`. Load imbalance
//     across P-cores and E-cores (Apple Silicon / Intel hybrid / Android
//     big.LITTLE) is automatically smoothed out.
//   * Caller participates as worker 0: `parallel_for` executes chunks on the
//     calling thread as well, so a 1-thread pool or small problem size pays
//     zero context-switch overhead.

#pragma once

#include "log3/core/fn_ref.h"
#include "log3/core/memory.h"
#include "log3/core/platform.h"
#include "log3/core/status.h"
#include "log3/core/types.h"

namespace l3 {

// Signature of a range kernel invoked by ThreadPool::parallel_for:
//   thread_idx : 0 for the calling thread, 1..num_threads-1 for pool workers
//   begin, end : half-open sub-range [begin, end) assigned to this invocation
using ParallelRangeFn = FnRef<void(Index thread_idx, Index begin, Index end)>;

// Signature of a 1D index kernel invoked by ThreadPool::parallel_for_index:
using ParallelIndexFn = FnRef<void(Index thread_idx, Index i)>;

class L3_API ThreadPool {
 public:
  struct Impl;

  // Create a thread pool with `num_threads` total threads (including the
  // calling thread; i.e., `num_threads - 1` background worker threads are
  // spawned). If `num_threads <= 0`, defaults to `cpu_features().physical_cores`
  // (clamped to [1, 64]).
  static Result<ThreadPool> create(
      int num_threads = 0,
      const Allocator& allocator = default_allocator()) noexcept;

  ThreadPool() noexcept : impl_(nullptr), allocator_(nullptr) {}

  ThreadPool(const ThreadPool&) = delete;
  ThreadPool& operator=(const ThreadPool&) = delete;

  ThreadPool(ThreadPool&& other) noexcept
      : impl_(other.impl_), allocator_(other.allocator_) {
    other.impl_ = nullptr;
    other.allocator_ = nullptr;
  }

  ThreadPool& operator=(ThreadPool&& other) noexcept {
    if (this != &other) {
      shutdown();
      impl_ = other.impl_;
      allocator_ = other.allocator_;
      other.impl_ = nullptr;
      other.allocator_ = nullptr;
    }
    return *this;
  }

  ~ThreadPool() noexcept { shutdown(); }

  // Total degree of parallelism (1 main thread + N-1 background workers).
  int num_threads() const noexcept;

  // Execute `fn(thread_idx, chunk_begin, chunk_end)` over `[begin, end)`.
  //   * If `end <= begin`, returns immediately.
  //   * If `grain_size <= 0`, automatically selects a balanced grain size
  //     (`div_ceil(end - begin, num_threads * 4)`).
  //   * Safe to call from multiple outer threads concurrently (serialized
  //     internally via a dispatch lock, or run inline if recursive).
  void parallel_for(Index begin, Index end, Index grain_size,
                    ParallelRangeFn fn) noexcept;

  // Convenience overload for per-index callbacks.
  void parallel_for_index(Index begin, Index end,
                          ParallelIndexFn fn) noexcept {
    parallel_for(begin, end, 0,
                 [&](Index tid, Index b, Index e) noexcept {
                   for (Index i = b; i < e; ++i) {
                     fn(tid, i);
                   }
                 });
  }

 private:
  void shutdown() noexcept;

  Impl* impl_;
  const Allocator* allocator_;
};

// Retrieve the global default ThreadPool (lazily initialized on first call,
// sized to `cpu_features().physical_cores`, thread-safe).
L3_API ThreadPool& default_thread_pool() noexcept;

// Top-level convenience wrapper using `default_thread_pool()`.
inline void parallel_for(Index begin, Index end, Index grain_size,
                         ParallelRangeFn fn) noexcept {
  default_thread_pool().parallel_for(begin, end, grain_size, fn);
}

}  // namespace l3


========== ./include/log3/core/memory.h ==========
// log3/core/memory.h
//
// Memory foundation: alignment math, overflow-checked size math, aligned
// allocation, the Allocator interface, memory statistics and small typed
// helpers. Tensor Storage, graph nodes, arenas and pools are all built on this.
//
// Conventions (the whole project follows them):
//   * Allocation failure is reported as nullptr, never by throwing. Callers
//     turn it into Status::error(StatusCode::kOutOfMemory, ...).
//   * Allocating 0 bytes returns nullptr, and deallocating nullptr is a no-op.
//   * alignment is always a power of two.
//   * Deallocation passes the original size and alignment ("sized
//     deallocation"). That lets pool/arena allocators pick the right size
//     class without storing a per-allocation header.
//   * Alignment defaults to kDefaultAlignment (64 bytes): one cache line, and
//     enough for aligned AVX-512 loads/stores on tensor data.
//
// Allocator is a plain struct of function pointers + context: no virtual
// calls through a vtable pointer chain, no std::function, no std::allocator,
// trivially copyable, constexpr-constructible, and cheap to pass by value.

#pragma once

#include <cstring>
#include <new>
#include <type_traits>
#include <utility>

#include "log3/core/assert.h"
#include "log3/core/platform.h"
#include "log3/core/types.h"

// Track allocation statistics in the default allocator. Costs two relaxed
// atomic adds per allocation; allocations here are large (tensors), so this is
// negligible, and peak-memory numbers matter a lot on edge devices.
#ifndef L3_MEMORY_TRACKING
#define L3_MEMORY_TRACKING 1
#endif

namespace l3 {

// Smallest alignment handed out for ordinary objects (not tensor data).
inline constexpr Size kMinObjectAlignment = 16;

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Alignment helpers (constexpr, header-only)
//   Preconditions: alignment is a power of two; align_up must not overflow.
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
constexpr bool is_power_of_two(Size x) noexcept {
  return x != 0 && (x & (x - 1)) == 0;
}

constexpr Size align_up(Size value, Size alignment) noexcept {
  return (value + alignment - 1) & ~(alignment - 1);
}

constexpr Size align_down(Size value, Size alignment) noexcept {
  return value & ~(alignment - 1);
}

inline bool is_aligned(const void* ptr, Size alignment) noexcept {
  return (reinterpret_cast<std::uintptr_t>(ptr) & (alignment - 1)) == 0;
}

// ceil(a / b) without the (a + b - 1) overflow. b must be non-zero.
constexpr Size div_ceil(Size a, Size b) noexcept {
  return a / b + (a % b != 0 ? 1 : 0);
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Overflow-checked size math. Return false (and leave *out untouched) on
// overflow. Use these for every size computed from user-provided counts.
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
inline bool checked_add(Size a, Size b, Size* out) noexcept {
  if (b > static_cast<Size>(-1) - a) return false;
  *out = a + b;
  return true;
}

inline bool checked_mul(Size a, Size b, Size* out) noexcept {
  if (a != 0 && b > static_cast<Size>(-1) / a) return false;
  *out = a * b;
  return true;
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Raw byte operations (null/zero-size safe: memcpy(nullptr, nullptr, 0) is
// undefined behavior in C, these are not).
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
inline void mem_copy(void* dst, const void* src, Size n) noexcept {
  if (n != 0) std::memcpy(dst, src, n);
}

// dst and src may overlap.
inline void mem_move(void* dst, const void* src, Size n) noexcept {
  if (n != 0) std::memmove(dst, src, n);
}

inline void mem_zero(void* dst, Size n) noexcept {
  if (n != 0) std::memset(dst, 0, n);
}

inline void mem_fill(void* dst, u8 value, Size n) noexcept {
  if (n != 0) std::memset(dst, value, n);
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Aligned allocation from the system (thread-safe)
//   Windows : _aligned_malloc / _aligned_free
//   POSIX   : posix_memalign / free (also available on every Android API
//             level; C11 aligned_alloc only exists from Android API 28)
// Returns nullptr on failure or when size == 0. Memory is NOT zeroed.
// Memory from alloc_aligned must be released with free_aligned.
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
L3_API void* alloc_aligned(Size size, Size alignment = kDefaultAlignment) noexcept;
L3_API void free_aligned(void* ptr) noexcept;

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Allocator interface
//
//   allocate_fn   : returns memory of at least `size` bytes aligned to
//                   `alignment`, or nullptr (also for size == 0).
//   deallocate_fn : receives the SAME size and alignment that were passed to
//                   allocate_fn. Never called with a null pointer.
//   ctx           : opaque state for the implementation (arena, pool, ...).
//   name          : static string for logs and debugging.
//
// Implementations must be safe to call from any thread unless documented
// otherwise (an arena used by one thread can say so in its own header).
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
struct Allocator {
  using AllocateFn = void* (*)(void* ctx, Size size, Size alignment) noexcept;
  using DeallocateFn = void (*)(void* ctx, void* ptr, Size size,
                                Size alignment) noexcept;

  AllocateFn allocate_fn;
  DeallocateFn deallocate_fn;
  void* ctx;
  const char* name;

  void* allocate(Size size, Size alignment = kDefaultAlignment) const noexcept {
    L3_DCHECK(allocate_fn != nullptr);
    L3_DCHECK(is_power_of_two(alignment));
    return allocate_fn(ctx, size, alignment);
  }

  void deallocate(void* ptr, Size size,
                  Size alignment = kDefaultAlignment) const noexcept {
    if (ptr == nullptr) return;
    L3_DCHECK(deallocate_fn != nullptr);
    deallocate_fn(ctx, ptr, size, alignment);
  }

  bool is_valid() const noexcept {
    return allocate_fn != nullptr && deallocate_fn != nullptr;
  }
};

inline bool operator==(const Allocator& a, const Allocator& b) noexcept {
  return a.allocate_fn == b.allocate_fn && a.deallocate_fn == b.deallocate_fn &&
         a.ctx == b.ctx;
}
inline bool operator!=(const Allocator& a, const Allocator& b) noexcept {
  return !(a == b);
}

// System allocator (alloc_aligned/free_aligned) with statistics tracking.
// Never null, valid for the whole program lifetime, thread-safe.
L3_API const Allocator& default_allocator() noexcept;

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Memory statistics of the default allocator (all zeros if
// L3_MEMORY_TRACKING is 0).
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
struct MemoryStats {
  u64 current_bytes;        // bytes currently allocated
  u64 peak_bytes;           // highest current_bytes since start / last reset
  u64 total_allocations;    // number of successful allocations
  u64 total_deallocations;  // number of deallocations
};

L3_API MemoryStats memory_stats() noexcept;

// Set peak_bytes back to current_bytes (measure the peak of one phase).
L3_API void reset_peak_memory() noexcept;

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Typed helpers
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

// Uninitialized storage for `count` objects of trivially destructible type T.
// Returns nullptr on failure, on count == 0, or if count * sizeof(T) overflows.
// Pair with deallocate_array using the same count.
template <typename T>
T* allocate_array(const Allocator& allocator, Size count) noexcept {
  static_assert(std::is_trivially_destructible_v<T>,
                "allocate_array returns raw storage; T must be trivially "
                "destructible (use create_object for other types).");
  Size bytes = 0;
  if (!checked_mul(count, sizeof(T), &bytes)) return nullptr;
  constexpr Size kAlign =
      alignof(T) > kDefaultAlignment ? alignof(T) : kDefaultAlignment;
  return static_cast<T*>(allocator.allocate(bytes, kAlign));
}

template <typename T>
void deallocate_array(const Allocator& allocator, T* ptr, Size count) noexcept {
  constexpr Size kAlign =
      alignof(T) > kDefaultAlignment ? alignof(T) : kDefaultAlignment;
  allocator.deallocate(ptr, count * sizeof(T), kAlign);
}

// Allocate and construct one object. Returns nullptr if allocation fails.
// T's constructor must not throw (the core is built without exceptions).
template <typename T, typename... Args>
T* create_object(const Allocator& allocator, Args&&... args) noexcept {
  constexpr Size kAlign =
      alignof(T) > kMinObjectAlignment ? alignof(T) : kMinObjectAlignment;
  void* mem = allocator.allocate(sizeof(T), kAlign);
  if (mem == nullptr) return nullptr;
  return ::new (mem) T(std::forward<Args>(args)...);
}

// Destroy and free an object made by create_object with the same allocator.
// Null is allowed.
template <typename T>
void destroy_object(const Allocator& allocator, T* ptr) noexcept {
  if (ptr == nullptr) return;
  constexpr Size kAlign =
      alignof(T) > kMinObjectAlignment ? alignof(T) : kMinObjectAlignment;
  ptr->~T();
  allocator.deallocate(ptr, sizeof(T), kAlign);
}

}  // namespace l3


========== ./include/log3/core/log.h ==========
// log3/core/log.h
//
// Leveled logging with pluggable sinks and compile-time stripping.
//
// Usage:
//   L3_LOG_INFO("loaded %d tensors in %.2f ms", count, ms);
//   L3_LOG_ERROR("matmul failed: %s", status.message());
//   l3::set_log_level(l3::LogLevel::kDebug);
//
// Design:
//   * Two filters. Compile-time: L3_LOG_MIN_LEVEL removes lower levels from the
//     binary completely (arguments are not even evaluated). Runtime: one
//     relaxed atomic load decides whether a message is built at all, so a
//     disabled log line costs a single compare-and-branch.
//   * Sinks are a plain function pointer + user_data (no virtual, no
//     std::function, no allocation). Built-in sinks: stderr and Android
//     logcat. The Python binding installs its own sink the same way.
//   * Sinks are invoked under an internal lock, so lines never interleave.
//     A sink must NOT log recursively (the implementation detects and drops
//     re-entrant calls).
//   * Logging never throws and never returns an error.
//   * L3_LOG_FATAL logs and then aborts the process.
//
// Compile-time level (set from CMake, e.g. -DL3_LOG_MIN_LEVEL=2):
//   0 trace, 1 debug, 2 info, 3 warn, 4 error, 5 fatal
//   default: 0 in debug builds, 2 (info) in release builds.

#pragma once

#include <atomic>

#include "log3/core/platform.h"
#include "log3/core/types.h"

#define L3_LOG_LEVEL_TRACE 0
#define L3_LOG_LEVEL_DEBUG 1
#define L3_LOG_LEVEL_INFO 2
#define L3_LOG_LEVEL_WARN 3
#define L3_LOG_LEVEL_ERROR 4
#define L3_LOG_LEVEL_FATAL 5

#ifndef L3_LOG_MIN_LEVEL
#if L3_DEBUG
#define L3_LOG_MIN_LEVEL L3_LOG_LEVEL_TRACE
#else
#define L3_LOG_MIN_LEVEL L3_LOG_LEVEL_INFO
#endif
#endif

namespace l3 {

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Levels
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
enum class LogLevel : u8 {
  kTrace = L3_LOG_LEVEL_TRACE,
  kDebug = L3_LOG_LEVEL_DEBUG,
  kInfo = L3_LOG_LEVEL_INFO,
  kWarn = L3_LOG_LEVEL_WARN,
  kError = L3_LOG_LEVEL_ERROR,
  kFatal = L3_LOG_LEVEL_FATAL,
  kOff = 6,  // only valid for set_log_level(): silences everything
};

constexpr const char* log_level_name(LogLevel level) noexcept {
  switch (level) {
    case LogLevel::kTrace: return "TRACE";
    case LogLevel::kDebug: return "DEBUG";
    case LogLevel::kInfo: return "INFO";
    case LogLevel::kWarn: return "WARN";
    case LogLevel::kError: return "ERROR";
    case LogLevel::kFatal: return "FATAL";
    case LogLevel::kOff: return "OFF";
  }
  return "?";
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Log record and sinks
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

// Everything a sink needs. All pointers are valid only during the sink call.
struct LogRecord {
  LogLevel level;
  u32 thread_id;       // small stable id of the calling thread
  u64 timestamp_ns;    // nanoseconds since the Unix epoch (wall clock)
  const char* file;    // source file (basename only)
  int line;
  const char* func;
  const char* message;  // null-terminated, no trailing newline
  usize length;         // strlen(message)
};

// A sink receives fully built records. It must be fast and must not log.
using LogSinkFn = void (*)(const LogRecord& record, void* user_data) noexcept;

struct LogSink {
  LogSinkFn fn;
  void* user_data;
};

// Built-in sinks.
//   log_sink_stderr : "[12:30:05.123] [INFO ] [t1] file.cpp:42: message"
//   log_sink_logcat : Android logcat (tag "log3"); no-op on other platforms
//   log_sink_null   : drops everything
L3_API void log_sink_stderr(const LogRecord& record, void* user_data) noexcept;
L3_API void log_sink_logcat(const LogRecord& record, void* user_data) noexcept;
L3_API void log_sink_null(const LogRecord& record, void* user_data) noexcept;

// Install a custom sink (fn must not be null). Safe to call at any time.
L3_API void set_log_sink(LogSink sink) noexcept;

// Restore the platform default: logcat on Android, stderr everywhere else.
L3_API void reset_log_sink() noexcept;

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Runtime level
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
namespace detail {

// Defined in src/core/log.cpp. Starts at kInfo.
L3_API extern std::atomic<u8> g_log_level;

}  // namespace detail

L3_API void set_log_level(LogLevel level) noexcept;
L3_API LogLevel log_level() noexcept;

// Hot-path check used by the macros: one relaxed load + compare.
L3_FORCE_INLINE bool log_enabled(LogLevel level) noexcept {
  return static_cast<u8>(level) >=
         detail::g_log_level.load(std::memory_order_relaxed);
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Emission (normally used through the macros below)
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

// printf-style. The message is truncated to kLogMaxMessageLength bytes.
L3_API void log_message(LogLevel level, const char* file, int line,
                        const char* func, const char* fmt, ...) noexcept
    L3_PRINTF_FORMAT(5, 6);

// Pre-formatted message (no format parsing, no truncation by the formatter).
L3_API void log_write(LogLevel level, const char* file, int line,
                      const char* func, const char* message,
                      usize length) noexcept;

// Flush/terminate path used by L3_LOG_FATAL: breaks into the debugger in
// debug builds, then aborts the process.
[[noreturn]] L3_API void fatal_abort() noexcept;

inline constexpr usize kLogMaxMessageLength = 1024;

}  // namespace l3

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Macros
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
#define L3_LOG_IMPL(level, ...)                                           \
  do {                                                                    \
    if (::l3::log_enabled(level)) {                                       \
      ::l3::log_message(level, __FILE__, __LINE__, __func__, __VA_ARGS__);\
    }                                                                     \
  } while (0)

#if L3_LOG_MIN_LEVEL <= L3_LOG_LEVEL_TRACE
#define L3_LOG_TRACE(...) L3_LOG_IMPL(::l3::LogLevel::kTrace, __VA_ARGS__)
#else
#define L3_LOG_TRACE(...) ((void)0)
#endif

#if L3_LOG_MIN_LEVEL <= L3_LOG_LEVEL_DEBUG
#define L3_LOG_DEBUG(...) L3_LOG_IMPL(::l3::LogLevel::kDebug, __VA_ARGS__)
#else
#define L3_LOG_DEBUG(...) ((void)0)
#endif

#if L3_LOG_MIN_LEVEL <= L3_LOG_LEVEL_INFO
#define L3_LOG_INFO(...) L3_LOG_IMPL(::l3::LogLevel::kInfo, __VA_ARGS__)
#else
#define L3_LOG_INFO(...) ((void)0)
#endif

#if L3_LOG_MIN_LEVEL <= L3_LOG_LEVEL_WARN
#define L3_LOG_WARN(...) L3_LOG_IMPL(::l3::LogLevel::kWarn, __VA_ARGS__)
#else
#define L3_LOG_WARN(...) ((void)0)
#endif

#if L3_LOG_MIN_LEVEL <= L3_LOG_LEVEL_ERROR
#define L3_LOG_ERROR(...) L3_LOG_IMPL(::l3::LogLevel::kError, __VA_ARGS__)
#else
#define L3_LOG_ERROR(...) ((void)0)
#endif

// Fatal is never stripped and never filtered: it always logs, then aborts.
#define L3_LOG_FATAL(...)                                                   \
  do {                                                                      \
    ::l3::log_message(::l3::LogLevel::kFatal, __FILE__, __LINE__, __func__, \
                      __VA_ARGS__);                                         \
    ::l3::fatal_abort();                                                    \
  } while (0)


========== ./include/log3/core/platform.h ==========
// log3/core/platform.h
//
// Foundation header. Detects OS, compiler, CPU architecture and compile-time
// SIMD level, and exposes portable attribute macros.
//
// Rules for this file:
//   * Zero #includes (no std, no system headers). Everything here is macros
//     and constants, so it is safe to include from anywhere.
//   * Every detection macro is always defined to 0 or 1, so use `#if L3_X`,
//     never `#ifdef L3_X`.
//   * L3_HAS_* macros describe what THIS translation unit was compiled for.
//     Runtime CPU dispatch (choosing AVX2 vs AVX512 etc. on the user's
//     machine) lives in a separate cpu-features module, not here.

#pragma once

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Version
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
#define L3_VERSION_MAJOR 0
#define L3_VERSION_MINOR 1
#define L3_VERSION_PATCH 0

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// C++ standard (MSVC reports it via _MSVC_LANG unless /Zc:__cplusplus is set)
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
#if defined(_MSVC_LANG)
#define L3_CPLUSPLUS _MSVC_LANG
#else
#define L3_CPLUSPLUS __cplusplus
#endif

#if L3_CPLUSPLUS < 201703L
#error "log3 requires C++17 or newer."
#endif

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Operating system
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
#if defined(_WIN32)
#define L3_OS_WINDOWS 1
#else
#define L3_OS_WINDOWS 0
#endif

// Android also defines __linux__, so it must be checked first.
#if defined(__ANDROID__)
#define L3_OS_ANDROID 1
#else
#define L3_OS_ANDROID 0
#endif

#if defined(__linux__) && !defined(__ANDROID__)
#define L3_OS_LINUX 1
#else
#define L3_OS_LINUX 0
#endif

#if defined(__APPLE__)
#define L3_OS_APPLE 1
#else
#define L3_OS_APPLE 0
#endif

#if !(L3_OS_WINDOWS || L3_OS_ANDROID || L3_OS_LINUX || L3_OS_APPLE)
#error "log3: unsupported operating system."
#endif

// POSIX-like convenience (everything except Windows).
#if L3_OS_WINDOWS
#define L3_OS_POSIX 0
#else
#define L3_OS_POSIX 1
#endif

// Keep <windows.h> (if someone includes it) from defining min/max macros.
#if L3_OS_WINDOWS
#ifndef NOMINMAX
#define NOMINMAX
#endif
#ifndef WIN32_LEAN_AND_MEAN
#define WIN32_LEAN_AND_MEAN
#endif
#endif

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Compiler
// clang-cl defines both _MSC_VER and __clang__: it is Clang, but uses the
// MSVC ABI/driver, so L3_MSVC_ABI is the right switch for MSVC-style syntax.
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
#if defined(__clang__)
#define L3_COMPILER_CLANG 1
#else
#define L3_COMPILER_CLANG 0
#endif

#if defined(__GNUC__) && !defined(__clang__)
#define L3_COMPILER_GCC 1
#else
#define L3_COMPILER_GCC 0
#endif

#if defined(_MSC_VER) && !defined(__clang__)
#define L3_COMPILER_MSVC 1
#else
#define L3_COMPILER_MSVC 0
#endif

#if defined(_MSC_VER)
#define L3_MSVC_ABI 1
#else
#define L3_MSVC_ABI 0
#endif

#if !(L3_COMPILER_CLANG || L3_COMPILER_GCC || L3_COMPILER_MSVC)
#error "log3: unsupported compiler."
#endif

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// CPU architecture
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
#if defined(__x86_64__) || defined(_M_X64)
#define L3_ARCH_X64 1
#else
#define L3_ARCH_X64 0
#endif

#if (defined(__i386__) || defined(_M_IX86)) && !L3_ARCH_X64
#define L3_ARCH_X86 1
#else
#define L3_ARCH_X86 0
#endif

#if defined(__aarch64__) || defined(_M_ARM64)
#define L3_ARCH_ARM64 1
#else
#define L3_ARCH_ARM64 0
#endif

#if (defined(__arm__) || defined(_M_ARM)) && !L3_ARCH_ARM64
#define L3_ARCH_ARM32 1
#else
#define L3_ARCH_ARM32 0
#endif

#define L3_ARCH_X86_FAMILY (L3_ARCH_X64 || L3_ARCH_X86)
#define L3_ARCH_ARM_FAMILY (L3_ARCH_ARM64 || L3_ARCH_ARM32)

#if !(L3_ARCH_X86_FAMILY || L3_ARCH_ARM_FAMILY)
#error "log3: unsupported CPU architecture."
#endif

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Compile-time SIMD level of this translation unit
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
#if defined(__SSE2__) || defined(_M_X64) || (defined(_M_IX86_FP) && _M_IX86_FP >= 2)
#define L3_HAS_SSE2 1
#else
#define L3_HAS_SSE2 0
#endif

#if defined(__AVX2__)
#define L3_HAS_AVX2 1
#else
#define L3_HAS_AVX2 0
#endif

#if defined(__AVX512F__)
#define L3_HAS_AVX512F 1
#else
#define L3_HAS_AVX512F 0
#endif

#if defined(__ARM_NEON) || defined(__ARM_NEON__) || defined(_M_ARM64)
#define L3_HAS_NEON 1
#else
#define L3_HAS_NEON 0
#endif

#if defined(__ARM_FEATURE_DOTPROD)
#define L3_HAS_NEON_DOTPROD 1
#else
#define L3_HAS_NEON_DOTPROD 0
#endif

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Pointer width
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
static_assert(sizeof(void*) == 8 || sizeof(void*) == 4,
              "log3: only 32-bit and 64-bit targets are supported.");

#define L3_64BIT (sizeof(void*) == 8)

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Build mode
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
#if !defined(NDEBUG) || defined(L3_FORCE_DEBUG)
#define L3_DEBUG 1
#else
#define L3_DEBUG 0
#endif

// Exceptions are NOT used inside the core. This only reports whether the
// compiler currently allows them (the Python binding layer may enable them).
#if defined(__cpp_exceptions) || defined(__EXCEPTIONS) || defined(_CPPUNWIND)
#define L3_HAS_EXCEPTIONS 1
#else
#define L3_HAS_EXCEPTIONS 0
#endif

// ThreadSanitizer detection. TSan does not model standalone atomic fences, so
// lock-free code that uses a fence on its fast path switches to an equivalent
// (slightly slower) ordering when this is 1. Normal builds are unaffected.
#if defined(__SANITIZE_THREAD__)
#define L3_TSAN 1
#elif defined(__has_feature)
#if __has_feature(thread_sanitizer)
#define L3_TSAN 1
#endif
#endif
#ifndef L3_TSAN
#define L3_TSAN 0
#endif

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Symbol visibility / DLL export
//   L3_STATIC     : building or using log3 as a static library -> no decoration
//   L3_BUILDING   : defined by CMake while compiling log3 itself
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
#if defined(L3_STATIC)
#define L3_API
#define L3_LOCAL
#elif L3_OS_WINDOWS
#if defined(L3_BUILDING)
#define L3_API __declspec(dllexport)
#else
#define L3_API __declspec(dllimport)
#endif
#define L3_LOCAL
#else
#define L3_API __attribute__((visibility("default")))
#define L3_LOCAL __attribute__((visibility("hidden")))
#endif

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Inlining and branch hints
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
#if L3_MSVC_ABI
#define L3_FORCE_INLINE __forceinline
#define L3_NOINLINE __declspec(noinline)
#else
#define L3_FORCE_INLINE inline __attribute__((always_inline))
#define L3_NOINLINE __attribute__((noinline))
#endif

#if L3_MSVC_ABI && !L3_COMPILER_CLANG
#define L3_LIKELY(x) (x)
#define L3_UNLIKELY(x) (x)
#else
#define L3_LIKELY(x) (__builtin_expect(!!(x), 1))
#define L3_UNLIKELY(x) (__builtin_expect(!!(x), 0))
#endif

// Mark cold paths (error handling, logging slow path).
#if L3_MSVC_ABI && !L3_COMPILER_CLANG
#define L3_COLD
#else
#define L3_COLD __attribute__((cold))
#endif

// Tell the optimizer a code path can never be reached.
#if L3_MSVC_ABI && !L3_COMPILER_CLANG
#define L3_UNREACHABLE() __assume(0)
#else
#define L3_UNREACHABLE() __builtin_unreachable()
#endif

// Pointer aliasing promise for hot kernels.
#if L3_MSVC_ABI
#define L3_RESTRICT __restrict
#else
#define L3_RESTRICT __restrict__
#endif

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Standard attributes (C++17)
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
#define L3_NODISCARD [[nodiscard]]
#define L3_MAYBE_UNUSED [[maybe_unused]]
#define L3_FALLTHROUGH [[fallthrough]]

// printf-style format checking: a wrong format string or argument type becomes
// a compile warning. fmt_index / first_arg_index are 1-based parameter
// positions (for non-static member functions, `this` counts as position 1).
#if L3_COMPILER_GCC || L3_COMPILER_CLANG
#define L3_PRINTF_FORMAT(fmt_index, first_arg_index) \
  __attribute__((format(printf, fmt_index, first_arg_index)))
#else
#define L3_PRINTF_FORMAT(fmt_index, first_arg_index)
#endif

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Alignment
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
#define L3_ALIGNAS(n) alignas(n)

namespace l3 {

// Typical x86-64 and ARM64 cache line. Use to avoid false sharing.
inline constexpr unsigned kCacheLineSize = 64;

// Default tensor storage alignment: one cache line, also satisfies AVX-512.
inline constexpr unsigned kDefaultAlignment = 64;

}  // namespace l3

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
#if L3_MSVC_ABI
#define L3_DEBUG_BREAK() __debugbreak()
#elif L3_COMPILER_CLANG
#define L3_DEBUG_BREAK() __builtin_debugtrap()
#else
#define L3_DEBUG_BREAK() __builtin_trap()
#endif

#if L3_MSVC_ABI
#define L3_FUNC_SIG __FUNCSIG__
#else

#endif

#define L3_STRINGIFY(x) L3_STRINGIFY_IMPL(x)

#define L3_CONCAT(a, b) L3_CONCAT_IMPL(a, b)

========== ./include/log3/core/str_view.h ==========
// log3/core/str_view.h
//
// StrView: non-owning, zero-allocation string slice (pointer + signed length)
// and high-speed 64-bit string/integer hashing primitives.
//
// Why not std::string_view or <string>:
//   * Zero <string> or <string_view> inclusion: avoids pulling in <iosfwd>,
//     <locale>, char_traits, and exception-throwing methods (.at(), .substr()).
//   * size() and indices use signed Index (i64), matching Span<T>, SmallVec<T>,
//     and tensor shapes across the entire log3 codebase. No npos unsigned traps
//     (kNotFound is -1).
//   * Built-in high-speed 64-bit Wyhash-inspired avalanche hash (`hash_bytes`,
//     `hash_str`, `hash_u64`) that processes 8 bytes per step instead of
//     byte-at-a-time FNV-1a, giving 4x-8x faster tensor name and BPE token
//     lookups in FlatMap.
//   * Constexpr-friendly, trivially copyable (two registers wide: 16 bytes on
//     64-bit), passed by value everywhere.
//
// Compatibility:
//   * C++17
//   * No dynamic allocation
//   * No exceptions
//   * No RTTI

#pragma once

#include <cstring>

#include "log3/core/assert.h"
#include "log3/core/platform.h"
#include "log3/core/span.h"
#include "log3/core/types.h"

namespace l3 {

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// constexpr-safe C-string length helper
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
namespace detail {

constexpr Index constexpr_strlen(const char* s) noexcept {
  if (s == nullptr) return 0;
  Index len = 0;
  while (s[len] != '\0') {
    ++len;
  }
  return len;
}

}  // namespace detail

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// StrView
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
class StrView {
 public:
  using value_type = char;
  using pointer = const char*;
  using const_pointer = const char*;
  using reference = const char&;
  using const_reference = const char&;
  using iterator = const char*;
  using const_iterator = const char*;

  // Sentinel returned by find*() when a character or substring is not present.
  static constexpr Index kNotFound = -1;

  // --- construction ----------------------------------------------------------
  constexpr StrView() noexcept : data_(""), size_(0) {}

  constexpr StrView(std::nullptr_t) noexcept : data_(""), size_(0) {}

  constexpr StrView(const char* data, Index size) noexcept
      : data_(data != nullptr ? data : ""), size_(size) {
    L3_DCHECK_GE(size, 0);
    L3_DCHECK(size == 0 || data != nullptr);
  }

  // Implicit construction from a null-terminated C string.
  // Passing nullptr constructs an empty StrView.
  constexpr StrView(const char* cstr) noexcept
      : data_(cstr != nullptr ? cstr : ""),
        size_(detail::constexpr_strlen(cstr)) {}

  // Construct from a Span<const char> or Span<const byte>.
  explicit constexpr StrView(Span<const char> s) noexcept
      : data_(s.data() != nullptr ? s.data() : ""), size_(s.size()) {}

  // --- observers -------------------------------------------------------------
  constexpr const char* data() const noexcept { return data_; }
  constexpr Index size() const noexcept { return size_; }
  constexpr Index length() const noexcept { return size_; }
  constexpr Size size_bytes() const noexcept { return static_cast<Size>(size_); }
  constexpr bool empty() const noexcept { return size_ == 0; }

  constexpr Span<const char> as_span() const noexcept {
    return Span<const char>(data_, size_);
  }

  ConstByteSpan as_bytes() const noexcept {
    return ConstByteSpan(reinterpret_cast<const byte*>(data_), size_);
  }

  // --- element access --------------------------------------------------------
  constexpr const char& operator[](Index i) const noexcept {
    L3_DCHECK_GE(i, 0);
    L3_DCHECK_LT(i, size_);
    return data_[i];
  }
